# Held vendor submissions

Submissions that arrived through `/submit-tool.html` (FormSubmit → `aitoolsessentials@gmail.com`)
and are **not published**. Each was reviewed independently and placed on hold pending
verification. A verification request was sent in almost every case; where the vendor publishes no
reachable address, the row says so explicitly — a bounced or unsendable request is **not** a request
on file. Hold means "not rejected", not "forgotten".

Rule: do not re-send a verification request for an entry that is already listed here with a
sent date, and do not publish a held tool until the vendor answers the outstanding questions.

Submitted **2026-10-23 13:15 UTC** (INBOX msg 194), category "Writing & Productivity" (**accurate**), `pricing_url` = `docs_url` = the homepage, contact `wangneo276@gmail.com`. **New lead, and a real, working product.** Free in-browser PDF→Markdown / Markdown→PDF / batch converter (Next.js on the OpenNext Cloudflare adapter, `x-opennext: 1`); domain registered **2026-10-19 08:53:32Z** (Namecheap), four days old at submission; Cloudflare NS `andronicus`/`isabel` (new to this ledger), **no MX record** (A records proxied), so mail to `@pdftomd.tools` would fall back to an implicit MX and die — the submitted Gmail is the only route. **The local-processing claim is verified from behaviour, not copy — the strongest positive in this submission.** Loaded `/` in headless Chrome with request logging, attached a test PDF to the real `input[type=file]`, and the tool returned `Quarterly Report Test Heading / Revenue grew twelve percent.` as Markdown. During conversion the only hosts contacted were `pdftomd.tools`, `umami.bestvid.net` (a 331/376-byte `/api/send` event) and `static.cloudflareinsights.com`; the PDF was read via same-origin `/pdf.worker.min.mjs` and a `blob:` URL, and **no request carried the file**. Matches the page's "Runs in your browser · nothing uploaded" and the shipped chunks (no upload call; `arrayBuffer()` + `URL.createObjectURL`). **Free with nothing to buy, and internally consistent:** Terms "It is free: there are no accounts, no payments and nothing to buy", `/llms.txt` "Free, with no account or sign-up", no price token, no checkout route on any fetched page — so there is no pricing claim to verify and no revenue surface at all. Legal set present at the `-policy` spellings (`/privacy` and `/terms` 308 to `/privacy-policy` and `/terms-of-service`; `/pricing`, `/docs`, `/refund-policy`, `/contact` 404), dated **2026-10-19 / 2026-10-20**. **Cluster: placed with the `lee`/`liberty` operator on two published signals, asked rather than asserted.** (1) The submission address is **the same Gmail `ailearnhub.net` publishes on `/pricing`** (that operator confirmed AILearnHub as one of its four products on 2026-10-20). (2) The Privacy Policy **self-declares ownership of the cluster's analytics host** — "we use Umami, a cookieless analytics tool we host ourselves at `umami.bestvid.net`" — and the script is live on every page (websiteId `a39de1c5-2383-46b0-b533-b95bdb3c75ef`, `data-tag="prod"`, `data-domains="pdftomd.tools,www.pdftomd.tools"`). The footer also carries the cluster's Ask-AI widget. Its NS pair is **not** `lee`/`liberty` (like `ailearnhub.net`, it is on a different pair), so the grade is contact + self-declared instrumentation, not nameserver. **Held on five items, all in the sent request:** (1) **no operator identity** — "the pdftomd.tools team" is the only identity anywhere, with no individual, company, country or postal address, and **no governing-law clause** in the Terms (only "the law that applies to you"); (2) **no contact address published at all** — every page says "Contact: we do not publish an email address yet", so a listing would carry no route, and we will not print the withheld Gmail; (3) **retention is a phrase, not a period and not a named provider** — "Request logs are kept by our hosting provider for a limited time under its own settings", while the served headers show Cloudflare/OpenNext; (4) **the Cloudflare Web Analytics beacon is undisclosed** — `static.cloudflareinsights.com/beacon.min.js` loads on `/privacy-policy` and `/terms-of-service` while the policy names Umami only and says there are no analytics cookies (a disclosure gap of the cookieless CDN-beacon class, not a hidden Google tag: zero `gtag`/`GTM-`/`fbq`/Clarity hits on the homepage); (5) **output ownership is unstated** — no clause says who owns the Markdown/PDF a user produces or grants a licence. **Name market, not a squat:** "PDF to MD" is descriptive and live unrelated products sit on five other domains — `pdftomd.app` (NS `jewel`/`toby`), `pdftomd.io` (`karina`/`kirk`), `pdf-to-md.com` (`duke`/`rosalyn`), `pdftomd.com` (Alibaba/HiChina) and `pdftomd.net` (One.com) — and **none of the three fetched siblings loads `umami.bestvid.net` or prints the operator's address**, so no family-ownership claim is made and no rights question is asked. **Positives to credit if it clears:** the working local-only tool, unusually plain copy ("no OCR … scanned files come out empty", "does not export images", "does not keep bold, italic, fonts or exact layout"), sources cited for claims (ISO 32000-2, GFM spec, pdf.js), 242 sitemap URLs across 11 language mirrors with `llms.txt`/`llms-full.txt`, and a policy that named its analytics host before we asked. Not published; no registries touched; no site page changed, so no deploy. |

**unverified named testimonials** on `/detector` attributed to named staff at Forbes, Stripe, HubSpot
and Nature Portfolio with no illustrative or early-access label.

Secondary open items: footer "SOC-2 Type II Certified" vs body "SOC 2-aligned controls"; the
98.7% accuracy / 1.2s / 12.4M+ documents / 142+ countries marketing figures are absent from their own
benchmark page, which reports precision 0.97 / recall 0.94 / F1 0.955 / FPR 1.2% for AIDetector.cx on a
400-document internal corpus; Paystack (Privacy §1) vs Stripe (Cookie Policy) for self-serve checkout;
retention covers analysis results (12 months) but not the submitted text itself; blogger-report credit
math (30 credits/report against Pro's 300/month); annual-plan eligibility and whether the 30% recurs on
renewals "for the lifetime of the customer" as `/affiliate-hub` states.

Verified independently, and **not** a squat or a microsite: aidetector.cx registered **2026-06-03**

read from the SPA bundle (`monthlyPrice` 12 / 29 / 79; annual $120 / $290 / $790). **Not part of the

`aidetector.net`, `aidetector.ai` (2016) — so any listing must carry the exact `.cx` domain; the newer

(`vedicastrologychart.net` links to blurbackground.vip + imagedescriber.dev + videoupscaler.video;

**`imagedescriber.dev` is not a single-product site; it is a multi-product operator with five published siblings.** Its footer links, in order, to: `allinai.tools` ("All in AI Tools" — an AI-tools
directory), `domainrank.app` ("Domain Rating" — an SEO/domain-analytics tool with a backlink

- `domainrank.app` runs on a **third** Cloudflare NS pair (`imani`/`ned`) and its own Cloudflare MX.

- So the three NS pairs (`pedro`/`sunny`, `alan`/`beth`, `ruben`/`rihana`) plus a fourth (`imani`/`ned`)

**Correction (2026-10-07):** this section previously read "the combined request would supersede those and
add vedicastrologychart.net and videoupscaler.video, which have holds but no outgoing request on record."
**Both do have requests on record** (msgs 107 and 109/110, each already naming the cluster question —
Vedic's asks verbatim whether it is operated by the same entity as imagedescriber.dev, unblurimage.me or
videoupscaler.video). The claim survived because nobody re-listed the Sent folder when editing the
paragraph; derive the per-domain request list from the mailbox, not from the surrounding prose. There is
now **no** genuinely unreachable contact in this group: `videoupscaler.video`'s published `support@`
still bounces (`550 5.1.1 address does not exist`, re-tested live 2026-10-07) while the address is still

SMTP `RCPT TO` — `250`, against `550` for a random local part), so the re-request went there
(**Sent msg 215**). A bounced request is not a request on file: the no-double-send rule protects
senders who received it, not a dead mailbox.

Any combined request should therefore be **held back** — resending to senders that already have requests
on file is exactly the double-send the ledger rule forbids — and if it is ever sent it should *add* the
two newly-found siblings `allinai.tools` and `domainrank.app`, which have **no submission on file**. Ask
whether those two are part of the same group rather than asserting it, since `allinai.tools` runs a
different NS pair and names a different governing law (USA).

runs several unrelated brands from one operator. Confirmed members (2026-10-06; the twelfth and thirteenth were added 2026-10-12, the fourteenth to sixteenth on 2026-10-20):

**Same operator, different NS pair — asked, not asserted: `ailearnhub.net`** (submitted 2026-10-20,
registered 2026-04-10). It is **not** on `lee`/`liberty` (its pair is `lindsey`/`syeef`, new to this
ledger) and it loads no `umami.bestvid.net` script, only a `<link rel=preconnect>` to that host. What
places it here is a **published artifact and copied policy text**: it links a Chrome Web Store
listing whose extension ID `dgihjimekmhphkbnnnomcbemhinmhmeg` is the identical one `adhdreading.org`
publishes (store title "ADHD Reading - Chrome Web Store"), its `/pricing` FAQ repeats that product's
Bionic Reading / Semantic Highlight / "Lifetime" / cloud-TTS copy, and `/privacy` describes browser
reading enhancements rather than lesson generation. Its submission contact is the same
`tianwei3333@agent.qq.com` as the rest. Keep it in the cluster question; do **not** file it as a
lee/liberty member on NS evidence.

All of them share the identical Cloudflare NS pair `lee.ns.cloudflare.com` / `liberty.ns.cloudflare.com`
and Cloudflare MX (`route1/2/3.mx.cloudflare.net`). Cross-links are bidirectional:
adhdreading.org → seedaud.io/productshotai.app/m3u8-player.net, and m3u8-player.net →
adhdreading.org/productshotai.app/seedaud.io; aiagentskills.net → the other three. This is
published by the operator, not inferred. **No member names a legal entity, data controller or postal
address**; on jurisdiction the sixteen now split — `tarotai.chat` names **Hong Kong** governing law and
`beadpattern.net` names the **People's Republic of China**, both with dispute paths, while the other
fourteen have no governing-law clause at all. Say which is which rather than repeating "none has a
governing-law clause".

**The operator has now answered — 2026-10-20 15:34 UTC, `support@aiproductads.app`, one reply covering
all four 2026-10-20 submissions** (All Mail msg 394; our request 14:54 UTC, our follow-up 15:57 UTC).
It confirms the relationship directly: "Yes. These four are one operator and four separate products",
gives the canonical address for each (ailearnhub.net, aiproductads.app, tarotai.chat, beadpattern.net),
and states "This note covers these four submissions only". That is a **vendor disclosure**, the
strongest grade in this ledger — file those four on it rather than on NS or instrumentation evidence.
`ailearnhub.net` is included in the operator's own account despite its different NS pair. On identity
the reply is a refusal, not an omission: "Each of the four sites is operated by an individual. There is
no registered company, company number, or postal address to publish, and we are not providing a personal
name." Jurisdictions are confirmed for `tarotai.chat` (**Hong Kong**) and `beadpattern.net` (**PRC**);
`ailearnhub.net` and `aiproductads.app` still name none. It also commits, for all four, that customer
inputs and generated results are **not used to train their own models**. The same reply says the
eleven other domains already queued here are outside its scope — so the cluster question is answered for
four products, not for the group: ask once more whether the same answers apply to the other eleven, and
to the two members with no submission on file (`m3u8-player.net`, `bestvid.net`).

facialharmonyai.com, aimangatranslator.app, digibouquetai.com, wan3video.art, aitextwatermarkremover.com,
musicvideoai.pro, **aiproductads.app and tarotai.chat** publish no cross-links, so they are placed here
by **shared infrastructure rather than disclosure**: same NS pair and same Cloudflare MX, plus every
page loading a self-hosted Umami analytics script from `umami.bestvid.net` (`bestvid.net` is the
cluster's one domain on Namecheap's `eforward1-5.registrar-servers.com` MX rather than Cloudflare's).
State the evidence, not a conclusion: ask each vendor whether it is related, rather than asserting it
is. **Two precisions to keep:** the instrumentation signature does **not** cover `seedaud.io`, and it
does **not** cover **`beadpattern.net`** either — both load no `umami.bestvid.net` script on any fetched
page, and `beadpattern.net` rests instead on the operator's *published* 友情链接 block (seedaud.io,
productshotai.app, m3u8-player.net), which is the stronger grade. `aiproductads.app` and `tarotai.chat`
both really do ship `umami.bestvid.net/script.js` (websiteIds `20ce005b-0c51-4e3b-a666-04015c46943f` and
`4-cd70-415a-9a39-0795541d1857`).
*(Re-confirmed 2026-10-12 by fetching the homepages: `aiagentskills.net`, `aitextcleaner.pro`,
`adhdreading.org` and `m3u8-player.net` all load `umami.bestvid.net`; re-fetched again 2026-10-20.)*

## Operator cluster: `hans`/`walk` NS pair — the Vidlark network (six submissions, one contact)

A fourth cluster — **separate from the three above** — arrived as **five FormSubmit submissions inside one
minute** (2026-10-16 02:26 UTC, INBOX msgs 126–130), all five carrying the **same contact address
`nullyangweiguang@gmail.com`**. FormSubmit timestamps carry **minute resolution only**, so assert the
batch but not any sub-minute spacing.

| Domain | Registered | Product | Sale model | MX | Reachable address |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **vidlark.ai** | 2026-10-10 | umbrella: free AI video generator (image/text-to-video) + ~13 trend landing pages | subscription, from $29.99/mo, 100 free credits | Zoho | support@vidlark.ai |
| **promptimage.cloud** | 2025-12-01 | AI image/video studio + prompt library | subscription, $29.99–$149.99/mo | Zoho | support@promptimage.cloud |
| **teslawraps.design** | 2026-09-22 | Tesla Paint Shop wrap designer (free editor + paid AI credits) | one-time packs $4.90–$19.90 | **Zoho triple** (gained since this row was written) | **none published at all** (`/about`, `/contact` 404, still) |
| **grokvideogenerator.dev** | 2026-10-04 | text/image-to-video on the Grok Imagine API | **subscriptions** $29.90–$99.00 list + credit packs | **none** | **none published** |
| **alorsondansevideo.com** | 2026-10-07 | two-photo studio dance video | one-time packs $9.90–$29.90 | **none** | support@alorsondansevideo.com (dead — no MX) |
| **badtimesai.dev** | 2026-10-14 | one-photo/group dance video | one-time packs $7.99–$49.90 | **none** | support@badtimesai.dev (dead — no MX) |
| **ifoundyouinthecrowdai.com** | 2026-10-14 | two-photo balcony-and-spotlight crowd video | one-time packs $7.99–$49.90 | **none** | support@ifoundyouinthecrowdai.com (dead — no MX) |

All seven resolve to the **same Cloudflare NS pair `hans.ns.cloudflare.com` / `walk.ns.cloudflare.com`**
(this pair appears in no other cluster in this ledger). **Six** of the seven have a submission on file;
`vidlark.ai` does not and is cluster context, not a lead. The NS pair was re-resolved for all seven rows
on 2026-10-16, so every domain in the table above is verified rather than inherited.

**Evidence for one operator, graded — the strongest line is the vendor's own published link, not the NS pair:**

1. **Published cross-link (proves one operator).** `promptimage.cloud`'s footer publishes
   "**Friends : Vidlark** · **Powered by : Vidlark**" linking `https://vidlark.ai`. And `vidlark.ai`
   itself publishes trend landing pages **`/bad-times-ai`** ("Bad Times AI Video Generator … the Bad Times
   trend (Imael Angel)") and **`/alors-on-danse-ai`** ("The Alors on Danse AI keyboard duo") — i.e. the
   umbrella markets **two of the five submitted products**. That is the operator disclosing the
   relationship itself.
2. **Shared Zoho MX triple** — `mx`/`mx2`/`mx3.zoho.com`, now identical on **three** of the seven:
   `vidlark.ai`, `promptimage.cloud` and `teslawraps.design` (which gained the triple after its row was
   first written — the earlier "two domains only" was correct when recorded and is stale now). A closer
   pairing than the NS pair; the remaining four have **no MX at all**, including the new
   `ifoundyouinthecrowdai.com`.
3. **Shared submission contact** on all five forms.
4. **Shared platform bundle (build-stack fact, NOT ownership proof).** All five microsites ship the same
   JSON i18n catalog of payment-settings labels — "`Stripe`/`Creem`/`PayPal`/`Alipay` payment gateway",
   "WeChat…", "Basic payment general settings" — the admin-panel strings of one multi-tenant platform.
   **Do not read those hits as a vendor's actual payment provider**: they are translation keys in a
   shipped bundle, not an integration claim, and writing "they use Creem and Stripe" from them would be
   fabricated. The same bundle evidence is how the `alan`/`beth` group was found to share the ShipAny
   boilerplate; it is a build-stack signal and belongs in the "are you related?" ask, not in a verdict.
5. **Shared house legal template — the same core text, not byte-identical pages.** All five microsites
   carry the operator's own Privacy body **verbatim sentence-for-sentence** ("We respect your privacy and
   are committed to protecting your personal data…"), and `vidlark.ai`'s own policy opens with the same
   paragraph. Whole-body `difflib` across the four comparable pairs is **0.651–0.855** because each
   microsite appends its own "First-party product analytics" section and two of them replaced the ToS
   wholesale — the ToS pairwise figure is **0.005–0.032** for `grokvideogenerator.dev` and
   **0.474–0.880** against the untouched template elsewhere. Say "one core text with per-site
   customisation"; **do not** call this a byte-identical-template cluster like `s07811005141`.

**Why this one matters operationally:** the operator owns a network of trend-clone AI video microsites
(`vidlark.ai` alone lists ~13 trend pages — Bad Times, Alors on Danse, Bulin 47, Raindance, Lego car spa,
Ghost filter, Broom trend, Toc Toc, Chinaza, Ceiling fan, Sway dance, Pet dance, Unforgettable car trend)
and has started submitting them individually. The real questions for the vendor are **whether they want
five separate listings or one** and **which surface a reader should be sent to**, not five separate
vetting passes. Ask once, combined.

**Not a trend-name race and not a squat.** "Bad Times", "Alors On Danse" and "Grok" are third-party trend
and model names rather than the operator's brands, and the sites mostly disclose that; there is no single
incumbent being fronted. The two name questions that *do* need asking explicitly are in the individual
rows: the **xAI/Grok** trademark (disclosed clearly by `grokvideogenerator.dev` — credit it) and **Tesla**
(`teslawraps.design` publishes Tesla model/trim templates with **no** non-affiliation line).

## Published vendor submissions (verified and live - do not re-vet as new)

A submission from a domain in this table is already listed. Check `data/tools.json` for the slug
before doing any verification work on it; a resubmission is not a new lead and must not be
re-published or re-asked. Publication is the expected outcome when ownership, pricing and legal
evidence hold up - a hold is for unresolved questions, not for imperfections. Both outcomes are
recorded: the hold table above for what is not live, this table for what is.

| Tool | Domain | Published | Submitted by | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Robylon | robylon.ai | 2026-10-16 | listings@robylon.ai | Vendor submission verified and published the same day, category reclassified from the submitted "Automation" to **AI Agents** on the verified use case. Operator identified in the notice itself (Zooid Tech Inc., San Francisco CA, with a postal address and phone) and corroborated independently by the domain's RDAP registrant matching the named co-founder and CEO; Delaware governing law, a refund policy, output ownership, input rights and a 30-60 day deletion window all read from the live Terms. Two items were published as **cons** rather than held, because neither implicates ownership: the Privacy Policy is dated 2026-04-07 and predates the current product, and the trust centre cited for the SOC 2 Type II and HIPAA claims (`trust.robylon.ai`) returns HTTP 403, so those claims stay vendor-stated. Reply sent 2026-10-17 04:13 UTC ("Your tool is live on AIToolsEssentials - Robylon"), one record in `[Gmail]/Sent Mail` and one in All Mail. Deliverability note: the domain is a Google Workspace **catch-all**, so an RCPT probe returns `250` for the published address and for a random local part alike - the address accepts mail, but the probe cannot confirm a human reads it. Cite this send by date + subject, never by Himalaya id. **Vendor acknowledged 2026-10-17 05:13 UTC** ("Re: Your tool is live on AIToolsEssentials - Robylon") — the three notes were passed to their team, together with the partner-track and linking questions. The reply asks nothing back and leaves no action pending, so there is no reply to send; a later pass should not re-read the thread as work. |
| GearDex | geardex.app | 2026-10-21 | geardex.help@gmail.com (submission form) | Published same day. Controller named inside the privacy notice (Dylan Andersen, Irvine CA, with a postal address), every processor named with purpose, GA4 behind Consent Mode defaulting to denied, prices agreeing across structured data, plan cards and the billing policy. Category reclassified from the submitted "Writing & Productivity" to **Productivity**. Policy describes iPhone/Android apps that do not ship and retention has no stated window - both published as `cons`, not held. |
| ImagesV2 | imagesv2.ai | 2026-10-22 | alex@imagesv2.ai (submission form); support@imagesv2.ai published | Published same day. Real live studio built around OpenAI's GPT Image 2 with a substantive privacy policy and ToS (named processors Stripe/Cloudflare/Vercel, retention periods of 12 months for IP signals and 5 years for payment records, GDPR + PRC PIPL rights with a 30-day target, non-affiliation with OpenAI stated in plain words), and pricing that reconciles across /pricing, /zh/pricing and llms.txt. Published with `cons` rather than held: no controller, entity, postal address or governing law named (the ToS clause is circular), Google Analytics 4 G-GC01M2BQCY and Microsoft Clarity load on the policy page with no consent gate against a sentence saying data is not transferred to outside parties, training use unstated, generated-image retention deferred to unpublished 'image storage terms', non-refundable with no refund page, subscription credits expire in 30-day blocks, and an always-on 'limited-time 50% off' banner. |
| CCC (Claude Command Center) | ccc.amirfish.ai | 2026-10-24 | amir.fish@gmail.com (submission form); amir@amirfish.com published on the parent site | Published same day; the submitted category "Development" was verified rather than taken on trust and kept. Free `$0` with no tiers, no account and no upsell, licensed **FSL-1.1-MIT** (copyright 2026 Amir Fish) — source-available, not OSI open source: no Competing Use, MIT on each version's second anniversary — v5.39.0, 202 stars, repository pushed the same day. Obtainability verified rather than assumed: `ccc.dmg` answers 200 at 3,614,187 bytes and the `amirfish1/homebrew-tap` formula answers 200. The telemetry claim was read in **source**, not from the policy copy: the beacon payload in `ccc_server/fleet_jobs.py` is listed field by field with a "never sent" list and a hard `CCC_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1` kill switch, the retired opt-in daily ping (2026-09-28) is documented in the same comment, and the site's `/analytics.js` shows PostHog with memory persistence, no autocapture, no session replay, no heatmaps and DNT honoured. Published with `cons` rather than held, because none of these is a reader-protection gate: **no privacy policy and no terms page** (`/privacy`, `/terms`, `/privacy.html`, `/terms.html`, `/legal/` all 404, and the parent `amirfish.ai` publishes none either — data practices live only in the repo's `docs/telemetry.md`, `SECURITY.md` and `docs/cloud-relay/SECURITY-AND-PRIVACY.md`), no company/postal address/governing law (the licensor is a named individual with a personal site naming Sunnyvale CA and a LinkedIn), PostHog loaded but named on no page, an anonymous beacon on by default with an env-var-only opt-out, and a no-auth server whose headless spawns use `--dangerously-skip-permissions` with world-readable state files. Name check: **crowded, not contested** — five unrelated repos share `claude-command-center` (nubbymong, Dicoangelo, sorlen008, afstkla, adamclark64) and no live incumbent trades as CCC; recorded in `cons`, not held as a rights dispute. `sitemap.xml`/`robots.txt`/`llms.txt` all 404. - **(1) GET the app's own public config endpoint — it is the strongest liveness and pricing surface a JS app offers.** Grep the shipped chunks for `/api/` strings, then fetch the config route with no account: `privatetryon.com/api/public-config` returned `billing: {enabled: true, checkoutReady: true, liveCheckoutReady: true}`, both packs and both Pro plans `active` with their prices, `customerAccess.freeCredits: 5`, the per-action credit costs, every feature flag, and PayPal as the sole provider — which settles "is this purchasable today" in one call, where the rendered pricing page only *claims* it. `/api/metrics` returning `{"ok":true,"data":{"enabled":true}}` confirmed the first-party stats claim at the same time.
- **(2) A provider-adapter registry in the bundle is evidence of what the code *supports*, not of what is configured — ask, never assert.** The shipped registry lists `agnes`, `agnes_image`, `agnes_video`, `gemini_image`, `bfl_flux`, `vertex_vto`, `fashn`, `fal_queue`, `replicate_prediction`, `openai_images`, `openai_compatible`, `custom_http_json` (with labels, capability lists and request templates, and one labelled "Agnes Image (legacy)"), while the policy names Agnes alone and adds "this confirmation is about Agnes. It does not describe a different provider." Write it as "these adapters exist in the code; which is live today, and will the policy name whatever is switched on" — the same whole-word-grade discipline as the `AWS`/`Redis`/`Crisp` subprocessor trap, applied to a signal that is real but partial.
- **(3) A CDN-injected analytics beacon sits on the policy page that denies it, and no tracker-name grep will find it.** Every fetched page — including `/privacy` and `/terms` — carries `static.cloudflareinsights.com/beacon.min.js` with `data-cf-beacon` (Cloudflare Web Analytics, cookieless, auto-injected when the zone enables it), against the privacy page's "we do not use third-party analytics … no third-party tracker". Sweep for `cloudflareinsights`/`data-cf-beacon` alongside the usual tracker list, and where the privacy page states the claim **the policy is also a tag-manager surface**. Grade it honestly: a cookieless CDN beacon on a Cloudflare-proxied site is a disclosure gap, not a hidden Google tag. Counter-example from the same pass: the only `gtag` hits in the bundle were minified core-js (`Symbol.toStringTag`), so print the surrounding bytes before reporting a tracker.
- **A pricing contradiction can be between the *live config* and the human page, not only between plan cards or two pages.** `/pricing` and `/runway-video` sell "10-second 720p runway video: 5 video credits" and "20-second … 10 video credits" (Pro's 5 credits "enough for one 10-second runway"), `/pricing.md` says "Video: 1 video credit(s) per clip", and `/api/public-config` serves `video: {durationSeconds: 5, resolution: 720p}` at `videoGenerate: 1` — a duration sold nowhere on the site. Where a machine-readable surface and the served config agree with each other against the marketing page, say which two agree rather than picking a winner.

So the domain carries exactly one reachable mailbox and its own Privacy/Terms/Refund pages publish only the dead `support@`. Request sent to `contact@` (**Sent msg 215**). Re-check findings: the legal set is now live and dated (`/privacy-policy`, `/terms-of-service`, `/payments-refund-policy`, all "Mar 31, 2026") with output ownership (ToS §7) and non-expiring credits (ToS §4); pricing read from `/pricing` **and its own JSON-LD `Offer` array** — packs 260 cr $15 / 900 cr $39 / 2,200 cr $79, monthly $15/$39/$79 for 300/1,000/2,500 credits, yearly $144/$374/$758. **Still open:** no entity/operator/jurisdiction and no governing-law clause; media providers unnamed (privacy names only Stripe + Creem, while the product pages advertise **Topaz / Topaz-Proteus** as the engine); retention and training use unstated; the cluster question; a live **estimator contradiction** (pricing's "Approximate jobs per package" panel 240/800/2000 vs allowances 300/1,000/2,500 vs the estimator's own 15-credit 30-second job); and **production theme placeholders** (`mailto:support@your-domain.com`, `discord.gg/your-app-name`, `twitter.com/your-app-name`). Domain registered 2026-05-21 (Spaceship; Cloudflare NS `pedro`/`sunny`) — a real product, not a squat. Do not re-send to `contact@`.

- **An SMTP `RCPT TO` probe is the cheap, authoritative way to test a vendor mailbox — use it before writing "unreachable" into a hold row.** Connect to the domain's own MX, `EHLO` as our domain, `MAIL FROM` our address, then `RCPT TO` the candidate address and read the reply code; **do not send any message**. `250` vs `550 5.1.1` distinguishes a live mailbox from a dead one in one call, and a random local part as a control (`550`) proves the domain is not a catch-all. This is how the ledger learned `contact@videoupscaler.video` works while `support@` bounces — a fact no published page states. It also avoids waiting three days for the implicit-MX fallback DSN.

- **Do not re-open or re-log a resubmission that the ledger already covers with a same-day re-check.** A parallel run can log the identical batch minutes after it lands; check `git log -1 --format='%ad' -- admin/held-submissions.md` against the mail's own `Submitted at` before writing anything. When the ledger is already current, the remaining work is *auditing* its claims, not restating them.

This payload *is* new inbound mail rather than a parallel-run duplicate: it arrived after the ledger's own commit (`git log -1 --format=%ad -- admin/held-submissions.md` → 2026-10-06 20:51 PDT), which is the check to run before deciding you are looking at someone else's work. Re-check findings: **the `/pricing` blocker is resolved** (the full paid matrix is now published — packs 100/300/800/1,500 cr at $4.99/$9.99/$19.99/$29.99, a $29.99/mo 600-or-3,000-credit subscription, API $99.99/11,000 cr and $199.99/25,000 cr) and **the refund terms are now findable** at `/payments-refund-policy` (note: *not* `/refund-policy`, and `/contact` + `/data-deletion` both 404 even though the site links them). **Still open:** no entity/controller/jurisdiction (ToS §11 "governed by applicable laws"); no named subprocessor ("trusted third-party providers for cloud infrastructure, AI model execution, basic analytics"); and a **new internal contradiction — ToS §4 says "Free / Standard plans: data is automatically deleted within 24 hours" against privacy's "removed after processing, with a one-day expiry as a cleanup fallback" plus 7-day/20-record local history, and no "Standard" plan exists on the published pricing.** Do not re-send.

Read the new FormSubmit body and diff it against the recorded one before repeating "identical payload"; the summary/name/contact can be unchanged while an URL field flips.

- **Verify a "multi-product operator" claim from the footer links, not from a shared nameserver — and expect the siblings' own legal pages to name different laws.** `imagedescriber.dev`'s footer links five siblings (`allinai.tools`, `domainrank.app`, `videoupscaler.video`, `vedicastrologychart.net`, `blurbackground.vip`). Of the two newly found ones, `allinai.tools` runs its **own** Cloudflare NS pair (`ruben`/`rihana`) *and* names a governing law (**USA**) that none of the six submitted domains does; `domainrank.app` runs a fourth pair (`imani`/`ned`) and names no country. So the relationship is carried by **published links**, and a family member naming a jurisdiction is not evidence the others do. Also: these two have **no FormSubmit submission** and no outgoing request, so they are cluster context, not leads.

Cheaper and more citable than driving a browser — and the browser helper may be unavailable when the local Chrome profile is locked.
- **A vendor's own benchmark page is the best source of an accurate number when the marketing block disagrees with it.** Their homepage/detector lead with "98.7% detection accuracy"; their `/research/ai-detection-accuracy-tests` reports precision 0.97 / recall 0.94 / F1 0.955 / FPR 1.2% and describes the corpus as internal. Quote the benchmark page's figures to the vendor and ask where the marketing number comes from, rather than challenging the headline directly — and never echo either figure into a listing.
- **"Certified" and "-aligned" are different claims; so are "Privacy §1" and "the Cookie Policy" within one site.** Both mismatches were found by grepping the same vendor's pages against each other, not by reading any single page. Sweep for `SOC`, `GDPR`, processor names (`paystack`/`stripe`/`supabase`/`resend`) and `train`/`opt out` across *all* their legal and marketing pages before concluding anything about their compliance posture.
- **A newer domain on the same brand name is not automatically the squatter — check the registration dates before framing it.** AIDetector.cx registered 2026-06-03; `aidetector.com` registered **2017-09-21** (Google Workspace MX), and `aidetector.net` / `aidetector.ai` also live. - **`spf=none` + `dmarc=fail` on an otherwise DKIM-passing Gmail-hosted send is normal for a Workspace-verified custom domain — but record it.** It is enough for editorial correspondence and not enough to authorise a change of payment destination on a partner's word alone.

Account required, browser-only, AI-written verse around names/occasion, own AI beat and vocals rather than the trend's recording. Effect pages carry dated changelogs ("October 14, 2026 The maker now takes two photos…", "October 11, 2026 The video now has sound"), which is a real editorial positive.

Checkout is wired, not announced: the live config reads `checkoutOpen:true`, `provider:"stripe"`, `mode:"prod"`, `pricesVisible:true`, `subscriptionsOpen:false`, `priceVersion:"rapduo-ladder-10cr-20261011"`. No purchase attempted.
  **Positives to credit in any eventual listing — the strongest disclosure set of any trend-effect submission so far.** Privacy, Terms, Refund, Acceptable Use and Cookie pages all 200 and dated 2026-10-11/13/15. The privacy policy names processors **by company and model** — Fal (Google Nano Banana image, ByteDance Seedance and Kuaishou Kling video), APIMart (named as the Hotel Lobby path), AWS Rekognition and OpenAI's moderation API, Cloudflare Workers + private R2, Supabase, Stripe — states **30-day** expiry for photos and outputs with the honest caveat that physical deletion runs on a scheduled job, says plainly it does not promise deletion from provider systems, keeps moderation verdicts/age estimates as records, describes the salted day-specific hash used for preview limits instead of storing IPs, and gates **named** analytics (Google Analytics, Plausible, Microsoft Clarity) behind consent with cookie names (`_clck`, `_clsk`, `fc_vid`, `fc_ft`, `fc_lt`, `fc_exp`, `fc_su`), masking rules, page exclusions and a working withdrawal path. Terms are 18+, require permission for other people's photos, bar celebrity and minor uploads, forbid presenting output as real footage, state outputs are AI-generated with no likeness guarantee, and carry an explicit no-affiliation paragraph (trend names describe a style; no artist, label, studio or other video app).
  **Why it is held — three items, all in the sent request:** (1) **the sale model contradicts itself across the site.** Terms: "Credits are sold in one-time packs, **or through optional monthly and yearly plans**", followed by auto-renewal and per-period credit-refresh mechanics; Refund Policy: "Credit packs **and plans**", "**Plans: cancel any time**", refunds of "any plan payment (**including renewals**)"; the pricing page's own FAQ answers the refund question with "unused packs and unused **first plan payments**" and "you can cancel a plan any time"; the structured data calls the product "Video credit packs **and plans**" (zh: "视频积分包与**订阅**"). Against that, the pricing page sells five one-time packs only, answers "Is this a subscription?" with "**No.** Each credit pack is a single payment… Nothing renews", and the live config reports `subscriptionsOpen:false`. **No plan price exists anywhere on the site**, so there is no figure to verify and none was guessed — the question sent is which model is authoritative and, if plans ship, their prices, periods and credit allowances. (2) **no operator identity or law:** the Terms and Privacy open with "Framecomet (framecomet.com, 'we')", `/about` says only "built and operated by a small independent team", and a sweep of every fetched page finds **no company, individual, country, postal address or company number**, and the Terms carry **no governing-law clause** (the only `law` hits are the liability limitation and "nothing… limits rights that cannot legally be excluded").   **Pricing verified from three surfaces that agree:** `/pricing`, its embedded plan payload and the `/zh/pricing` mirror all give 10 credits **USD 2.99**, 30 **USD 6.99**, 100 **USD 14.99**, with struck-through list prices of **USD 4.99 / 9.99 / 24.99**, `interval:"one-time"`, `valid_days:0` (never expire), `payment_providers:["stripe","creem"]`, 1 credit per try-on and 1 free credit at signup. The per-credit arithmetic reconciles (about USD 0.30 / 0.23 / 0.15). **No subscription exists** — the 20 `Stripe`/`Creem`/`PayPal`/`Plausible`/`Clarity` hits in the shipped bundle are i18n keys for the template's admin settings panel, checked in context, and there is no recurring flag anywhere; do not re-open "subscriptions" from those strings. Checkout was not reached: an account is required, and no signup or purchase was attempted, so the record says so rather than implying a live session was seen.
  **The strongest finding — a face-image privacy promise contradicted by the product on the same page.** Upload box: "Your photo and camera are used only for this try-on". FAQ, also emitted as FAQPage structured data: "Your photo is used only for this session's analysis and try-on generation. We don't use it for training or share it." Against that, the same homepage advertises "History — Every try-on is saved automatically. Revisit, compare, and share with friends", `/about-us` offers to "Save and compare different eyewear styles", `/pricing` sells downloadable hi-res renders, and the Privacy Policy retains data "for as long as reasonably necessary" while naming "cloud databases, AI model providers, … object storage". The word **`train` appears nowhere in the policy or the Terms**, while the Terms grant a licence to process uploads "for providing, operating, maintaining, and improving the service".
  **Second finding — tags firing on the page that denies collection.** The root layout injects `googletagmanager` for Google **Ads `AW-18136694057`** and **Microsoft Clarity `x12jqf0qok`** with `strategy:"lazyOnload"`; both are present in the served HTML of `/privacy-policy` itself, the policy names neither, and the site has **zero** consent/cookie-banner strings. Grade it a disclosure gap (they are conversion and session-recording tags rather than hidden trackers), and keep the citation on the policy page because that is where it is strongest.
  **Published as `cons`:** no controller identity and no governing law (no company, individual, country, postal address or company number on any fetched page; no governing-law/jurisdiction/dispute/arbitration clause); processors unnamed and retention a phrase rather than a period; no clause assigning rights in the rendered try-on image (`/refund-policy` and `/refunds` also 404, with consumed credits "generally non-refundable" and no EU/UK withdrawal statement); a struck-through list price on all three packs, i.e. an always-on discount; `/zh/pricing` quoting per-credit rates in yuan against dollar totals, with CNY amounts (9.9 / 19.9 / 59.9) in the config that the page never shows; a free tier of exactly one credit; and legal text that refers to "subscriptions" and "paid plans" the pricing page does not sell.
